ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-23.u54	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Dear Mr. Chairman, honourable members of the government, honourable Members of the National Assembly! I think that there is no person in this Chamber who is convinced that this is the first and last view of the Law on the Budget of the Republic of Bulgaria for 2022. If you remember, just a month ago, we adopted a Law by which we extended certain norms for the first quarter to 31 March 2022. Even after the signing of the coalition agreement, the coalition partners stated strongly that from 1 July there will be an update of the state budget, i.e. until 30 June this update will be submitted to the National Assembly in order to be approved and to enter into force from 1 July 2022. This is indeed a precedent in our history before the basic budget law has been adopted, to talk about its update, which shows in practice that this bill, which we are looking at today, does not have an annual time character. Dear Minister of Finance, if you recall, and I am sure you remember, at the meeting of the Budget and Finance Committee, we have made a number of requests. Of course, most of them have been rejected, but some of them have been accepted as appropriate by you and you have taken up the commitment in the coming months to work on these proposals and with the budget update to decide whether to accept them and what the cost of these proposals will be as a reflection in the budget for 2022. The same procedure has taken place in a number of other committees in which the committees have accepted as appropriate proposals, but have accepted that this can happen with the update of the budget. Yesterday, in view of the draft law, of course, the State Social Security Budget Act, the President of the Commission from this tribune, defending the finally adopted Act on the budget of the DVO for 2022, said that this year there will be an enormous amount more revenue than is foreseen in the consolidated fiscal programme, and of course I assume that it is based on the high inflation that will bring more revenue into the state budget. We are in a very complicated situation from which we can come up in one of the following two ways. More than two months have passed since the coalition agreement between coalition partners was signed. There remains yet more than a month, a month and ten days until the end of the extension of the provisions of the 2022 budget, which will act until 31 March this year. For four months, I think it is absolutely enough to make the coalition agreement between coalition partners into a management program. Therefore, my proposals are two, which of the two you choose, of course. The first is to withdraw the government Bill on the state budget of the Republic of Bulgaria for 2022 and by the end of March to submit the real budget, which will have an annual horizon of action. During this time, the updated medium-term budgetary forecast will also be able to be corrected, the policies, priorities and reforms you are talking about, during the three-year period and look at a really annual law on the budget of the Republic of Bulgaria for 2022. The second option is, I am sure that the first option will not be accepted because this will really be a precedent, the second option is that this law is not to be called the State Budget Act of the Republic of Bulgaria for 2022. If you read in your motives in the updated medium-term budget forecast, you yourself have said that this is a basic version of the budget and not year-round, with all the policies and priorities that you have provided for. So my proposal, Mr. President, is that if the government does not accept to withdraw its budget for 2022 and to present it by 31 March then, when the first bill that we have drawn up expires, the name of this budget will be the State budget of the Republic of Bulgaria for 2022. Thank you.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-23.u73	Thank you, Mr. Karimansky. I'm opening the discussion under art. 1. Hristo Terziyski is the first recorded speaker. Here you go, Mr. Terziysky.
ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-23.u74	Dear Mr. President, ladies and gentlemen of ministers, honourable Members! I take the floor to argue the proposal of our parliamentary group for an increase in the budget of the Ministry of Interior. There are topics and areas on which continuity and sustainability in policies are appropriate, regardless of who manages the country. This is a matter of national security and of public interest, and this topic is precisely one of them. Employees in the structures of the Ministry of Interior enjoy institutional and public support in all democratic countries, and to an even greater extent this also applies to the member states of the European Union. That's understandable. They are relied on in every challenge against the security of citizens and public order. It is a profession that is associated with the highest risk to the life and health of employees in the performance of their duties. Each prosperous country takes care of the adequate standard of living and the public prestige of these employees. Unfortunately, however, with a budget of 2022, we see that Bulgaria will become an alarming example of an exception to this principle. Let's not let that happen, it's up to all of us. In the proposal we have tabled, we have made a clear estimate of the minimum necessary resources of the main items related to the expenditures of the Ministry of Interior. The 15% increase proposed by us for the remuneration of the employees would continue the trend that we have started in the past years and which we have implemented with a budget of 2021. Let us not forget that those working in the Ministry's system are among the most vulnerable to inflationary pressure public officials due to legal limitations and practical inability to side employment and side incomes. At the same time, the employees of the Ministry of Interior are among the most exposed to corruption pressure and in order to effectively counter such attempts and to be able to perform their duties conscientiously and effectively, their standard of living must be ensured by a decent minimum. That is why the last three regular Bulgarian governments have constantly been making efforts to raise their payments. Last but not least, in their daily commitments to prevent and counter crime, to protect public order in security and other functions, the uniformed officers of the Ministry of Interior practically represent the person of the state. Ladies and gentlemen of the Parliament, concern in our country is not only the lack of funding of the Ministry of Interior, but also the mistrust and negative messages that are addressed to those working in the system by the high levels of government. It is insulting and greatly damaging to the authority of the thousands of decent servants. Recently the prime minister pointed out that the new anti-corruption agency will be sought from abroad because those in Bulgaria were related to the persons to be investigated. Shortly after that, the government spokeswoman saw paid provocations in legitimate and fair demands and the protest readiness claimed by the police syndicates, and subsequently attempted to overwhelm this gaffe with the explanation that she misunderstood and got confused. ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-23.u145	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Dear Mr. Karimanski, you said that the proposals made with a source of funding on high income from value added tax would break the fiscal rule that costs should be up to 40%. Yes, it is, there is such a fiscal rule, but this fiscal rule is calculated on the basis of a basic gross domestic product, and gross domestic product, which is not as a provision in the State Budget Act. If it were a legal provision, all our proposals would also include updating the gross domestic product. We believe that by about 5% is the deflator of gross domestic product and by 1% is overrated growth. So net 4% we have an undervalued deflator, which means that the costs can be 2 billion more and get into the 40% rule. Another point is that now they are breaking it with some conventions, if we exclude the cost, if we exclude the cost of pensions, we have no problem at all. The problem with this budget and what you are saying is that we can really come into the hypothesis of breaking this fiscal rule, but we are breaking it because we have previously broken the basic principles of good budgetary management. Basic principles laid down in the Public Finance Act. These are principles such as budget yearliness, principles such as sustainability, predictability of public finances. That saying at the beginning of the year that you will make an update, initially bet an update without any new circumstances, is the most striking violation of these principles of public finances. That is where the violation of the fiscal rule comes from, because if the budget was correct in its forecasts, the gross domestic product would probably be around 149 lv. And we wouldn't be talking about breaking the fiscal rule. Thank you.

ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-23.u225	Dear Mr. Chairman, honourable Members! We object to the way in which the draft budget is proposed concerning the sector.......................................................................................... It reflects the structural coalition agreement of the current government by four parties, but does not comply with the current structure and system of the Ministry of Agriculture, respectively, with the current legal norms. The proposal for a BABH budget under the current legislation and the lack of any support for the separation of the Bulgarian Food Safety Agency from the Ministry of Agriculture is extremely disturbing. The principle of European Union legislation is uniform control from farm to table. The official controls in the Republic of Bulgaria within the meaning of European Union legislation are carried out by one state body and this is the Bulgarian Food Safety Agency. The Ministry of Agriculture is the body that creates the overall agri-food chain management policies from the farm to the table, sets up implementing acts for these policies, manages the process of carrying out an independent risk assessment for each of the processes by another body. The effective functioning of the system is also determined by the single and political leadership in the face of the Ministry of Agriculture. Any non-financing of the system in any part carries the risk of a problem to ensure animal health and food safety. The same risk would be present in any non-saccharatable action or management of the processes of each of the state structures, part of the management and control system throughout the agri-food chain from the farm to the table. Effectiveness can be ensured in the case of single process management. The activity under the leadership of a single principal ensures effective coordination and control throughout the agricultural chain process from the farm to the table, enabling the single body to guide and monitor at all stages from the birth of the animal or plant, its healthy cultivation to the production of the products ready for human consumption and the establishment of an appropriate budget to ensure the implementation of this process. This provides a greater guarantee for the safety of food consumed by people, and hence a guarantee for their health. At present, the Ministry of Agriculture is implementing this single state policy, as it is a single principal of all food safety services. They are under his jurisdiction: State Fund........................................................................................................................................................................................................................................................... All these bodies are directly related to the agri-food chain and food safety in the single process. Each stage of the agri-food chain process carried out by these organs contributes to animal and plant health and hence to the safety of food consumed by humans, respectively their health. In order to ensure the safety of a particular food, it must be controlled by animal farming, the plants, respectively, throughout the farm's control process, until the actual receipt of the relevant food at the human table and correspondingly financially secured. Guided by the Single Principle, which creates and manages policies and evaluates the necessary means for the implementation of this whole process, the possibility of ensuring uniform legislation, a single policy and control over food safety shall be made available. The coordination and close relationship with the State Fund is of particular importance as it lays down the terms and conditions for financial support and the granting of the corresponding subsidies to farmers in these sectors. The fragmentation of this process would lead to the fragmentation of policies, legislation and control of the process of ensuring food safety along the chain, and hence also the risk to people's health. The Minister of Agriculture, as a central sole body of the executive, shall direct, coordinate and supervise the implementation of the single state agricultural, rural, forestry and hunting policy, fisheries, aquaculture and conduct a single state food safety policy in accordance with the legislation in force throughout the agri-food chain and shall assess the means necessary for its implementation. The transition of the Bulgarian Food Safety Agency to the Council of Ministers alone carries the risk of fragmentation of the entire process of conducting agri-food policy and hence reduces the effectiveness of control in the stages and processes of the agri-food chain and food safety. [[The President signals that time has expired.]] In this spirit is also the European legislation, which in Regulation 625/2017 united into a single law... [[Noise and lines.]] By unifying the process into a single body of state policy in the field of agriculture and food safety, the entire process of the agri-food chain to the Ministry ensures the implementation of the common economic and food safety policy in the European Union. [[The President repeats the signal that time has expired.]] The separation from the Ministry of Agriculture only to the BABH budget, except that it is inappropriate for the above reasons, but will result in a lack of effective control of the agri-food chain. It's also totally illegal. First of all, this contradicts the Public Finance Act and the Bulgarian Food Safety Agency Act.
ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-23.u226	Mr. Stankov, you're one minute over time. Please, please! I'm only giving you one minute more. Please, one last sentence.
ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-23.u227	In this case, the Law on the Bulgarian Food Agency is an authorising officer of a budget to the Minister of Agriculture. In accordance with art. 12 of the same law funds for the activities of BABBH are provided annually through the budget of the Ministry of Agriculture. The Budget Act cannot contradict the Public Finance Act and other laws included in the BABH Act. The budget law for the financial year concerned lays down the budget of already established structures and cannot restructure budgetary organisations contrary to the laws under which they are established. The same must be in full compliance with the Public Finance Act, the laws defining the structure of budgetary organisations. ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-23.u1254	Let's stop by art. 3, Mr. Karimansky. Thank you. I'm opening a discussion on art. Art. 2 and Art. 2 3. Is there anyone who wants to make a statement? Here you are, Mrs. Alexandrova.
ParlaMint-BG_2022-02-23.u1255	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Dear Minister, dear colleagues! My statement is under art. 2 of the so reported draft budget and with regard to the proposal of the MP Mr Karimanski and a group of MPs. I will express my negative attitude to this proposal, which significantly reduces the budget of the judiciary. Although the proposal to amend the Bill is linked to a reduction of the planned current expenditure on the budget of the judiciary for 2022 in the part of the Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Bulgaria, there will be a result of failure to carry out in full the activities of all units of the judiciary. In this regard, I would like to draw attention to the fact that art. 117, para. 3 of the Constitution of the Republic of Bulgaria explicitly proclaims an independent budget of the judiciary as a condition for its independence and functioning. This calls for sufficient resources to be provided for the normal functioning and normal activity during the budgetary year, and therefore their lack constitutes a failure to comply with a constitutional rule on independence of the judiciary budget and is, in fact, an infringement of its independence. The Constitutional Court of the Republic of Bulgaria has created a lasting and uncontroversial practice in solving a number of similar problems in previous years and has ruled that any law on an annual state budget allowing a financially unsecured budget for the judiciary creates a danger of blocking the activity and leads to inequality of the divided authorities and is contrary to the one established in art. 8 of the Basic Law principle. In view of the foregoing, we believe that the proposed amendment to the state budget is an expression of a dangerous trend of pressure on the judicial authorities through the methods and means of the executive and legislative authorities, which is unacceptable in a rule of law, a sharing and developing state like ours. Such a decision we believe will lead to blocking the work in the judiciary, inability to fulfil the duties of the judicial authorities, and will directly undermine the rights of the Bulgarian citizens. We believe that the provision of one type of activity financed by the state budget at the expense of another, which is what you are doing, is putting one institution against another institution, that is, this 20 million you are transferring them to the Bulgarian Academy of Sciences, which is wonderful, of course, but not in this sense, and in that order, in which you are doing it, is a failure to fulfil the obligations of the legislative authority. You're trying to do something, and it's really nothing. We find it unacceptable to bind the funds for the current costs of the prosecution with the need for its urgent reform and restructuring, dear Minister, in order to achieve greater efficiency. The same definition as sanctioning the prosecution with unacceptable motives, which is not only in violation of the basic constitutional principles, but also intolerable placing under the condition of the rule of law in Bulgaria and is a direct attempt to influence and subjugate the structures of the judiciary by the other state authorities and cannot be accepted in a democratic society part of the European legal area. I will conclude by quoting the opinion of the Advisory Council of Judges in Europe to the European standards of independence of the judiciary: The Advisory Council considers and is of the opinion that although the financing of the courts is part of the state budget submitted to Parliament by the Ministry of Finance, this funding should not depend on political turmoil. Although what level of funding the courts can afford each state is a political decision, it must always be taken care of in a system based on the separation of authorities, executive and legislative powers not being able to put pressure on the judiciary in determining its budget. This is the opinion of the Advisory Council of Judges in Europe. The decision on the allocation of funds to courts must be taken in the strictest respect for the independence of the judiciary. Thank you.
